Let me reconsider. In human nomenclature:
- The standard nomenclature from the ATG8 family committee (Kaufmann et al., 2014) uses:
  - ATG8A = MAP1LC3A (LC3A)
  - ATG8B = MAP1LC3B (LC3B)  
  - ATG8C = MAP1LC3C (LC3C)
  - ATG8D = GABARAP (GABARAP)
  - ATG8E = GABARAPL1 (GABARAPL1/GEC1)
  - ATG8F = GABARAPL2 (GABARAPL2/GATE-16)

There are only 6 ATG8-family proteins in humans. So ATG8G and ATG8H don&#x27;t exist in humans.

But in plants like Arabidopsis, there are more ATG8 genes:
- ATG8a, ATG8b, ATG8c, ATG8d, ATG8e, ATG8f, ATG8g, ATG8h, ATG8i

So if this is about plant proteins, we could have ATG8G and ATG8H.

Actually, in Arabidopsis, the ATG8 family is divided into two clades:
- Clade 1: ATG8a, ATG8b, ATG8c, ATG8d (interact with most AIGs)
- Clade 2: ATG8e, ATG8f, ATG8g, ATG8h (interact with specific AIGs)

Some specific AIGs (ATG8-interacting GABARAP-like proteins) preferentially interact with clade 2 ATG8 proteins (ATG8e-h), while others prefer clade 1 (ATG8a-d).

Phytosulfokines are sulfated tyrosine-containing peptides. They are plant peptide hormones that contain sulfated tyrosine residues. The tyrosine residues in phytosulfokine precursors are post-translationally modified by sulfation (addition of sulfate groups to the hydroxyl group of tyrosine).

PSKs are pentapeptides with sulfated tyrosine residues at positions 2 and 4 of the mature peptide. The precursor proteins contain tyrosine residues that undergo tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase-mediated sulfation.
